  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Rocketry: Allows you to build the Guided Missile, a one-time use unit specialized in damaging City garrisons.
        Completing it would unlock: Nuclear Fission, Radar
      
      ## Policies
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 5
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 8
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 8
        - WarmongerHate: 6
        - NeutralBias: 7
        - FriendlyBias: 4
        - GuardedBias: 7
        - AfraidBias: 5
        - DiplomaticBalance: 6
        - Friendliness: 5
        - WorkWithWillingness: 8
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 8
        - Loyalty: 9

        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 4
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 4
        - MinorCivWarBias: 3
        - DenounceWillingness: 8
        - Forgiveness: 3
        - Meanness: 9
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 8
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Portugal is 4/7 toward cultural victory with America just influenced. Rome at 81% (19 turns) and me at 63% (44 turns) from being influenced. Maximum WarBias (10), VictoryCompetitiveness (10), and Boldness (10) ensures aggressive prosecution of war against Portugal while simultaneously racing for science victory. High Meanness (9) and HostileBias (8) prioritize damaging Portuguese tourism infrastructure. Must vote FOR Venice's Sanction: Portugal proposal in World Congress to block Portuguese trade routes and tourism.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Rocketry
        - Rationale: Robotics completes in 12 turns. Rocketry is the clear next choice as it unlocks Guided Missile and leads to Nuclear Fission → Advanced Ballistics. Nuclear Fission critical for Nuclear Power Plants (+50% production) which will significantly boost spaceship part production across my 14-city empire. The Advanced Ballistics → Rocketry → Satellites → Apollo Program path is essential for science victory. Radar later unlocks for Stealth Bombers if needed against Portuguese infrastructure, but science victory takes precedence over military options.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Iron Curtain (Policy)
        - Rationale: Policy available in 13 turns. Iron Curtain remains the optimal choice for emergency science victory acceleration. With 14 cities and established internal trade route network, the +200% Food or Production bonus from internal trade routes will generate massive output - potentially 600+ extra production per turn across the empire. Free Courthouse upon immediate city annexation saves critical gold for rush-buying spaceship parts. +5 Gold and +5 Production from city connections stacks across all 14 cities. This is superior to Spaceflight Pioneers at this stage because production infrastructure must be established before Great Engineer rushing becomes viable.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Vatican City
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Vatican City relations to neutral 0/0 from 40/45. They are Ottoman allies fighting our enemies which helps us, but they are a distant city-state with minimal strategic value to our core interests. With two major wars ongoing, we need to focus diplomatic resources on immediate concerns rather than maintaining elevated relations with peripheral actors. This is diplomatic triage.
        - UpdatedTurn: 190
        
        ### Tyre
        - Public: -20
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: Negative relations with Tyre (-20/-25) after Ethiopia used Great Diplomat to flip them to Ally status, dropping my influence to -5. Tyre is now Ethiopia's ally (my vassal), which complicates the relationship. I should not waste diplomatic resources contesting this - let my vassal Ethiopia have Tyre. Maintaining negative relations signals the AI not to prioritize diplomatic missions or envoys to Tyre, focusing instead on more strategic city-states like Luba, Hanoi, and Marrakech where I maintain strong alliances.
        - UpdatedTurn: 211
        
        ### Luba

With Ottomans as our crucial defensive pact allies fighting alongside us, maintaining strong positive relations with their city-state allies prevents diplomatic friction. Luba has declared war on our enemies (Portugal), providing military value. Increased relations signal our AI to coordinate diplomatic efforts with Ottoman interests and potentially secure joint envoy missions.

This signals my AI not to waste further diplomatic resources or envoys on Hanoi since Venice has secured permanent ally status. Negative relations prevent automatic assignment of diplomats/envoys to this now-lost cause, focusing resources on city-states I can actually influence like Luba, Vancouver, and Marrakech.

Focus resources on city-states where I maintain positive standing like Vatican City, Vancouver, and Almaty.

Setting relations to -40/-45 matches other hostile city-states at war with me (Ban Chiang, Djenne, Bratislava) and signals the AI not to waste diplomatic resources, envoys, or missions on this hostile actor until peace is possible.

Combined with Marrakech's 842 Science quest, these two quests could give me 65-70% of a technology - essential for accelerating toward Information Era before Portuguese cultural victory. High relations (90/95) ensure quest priority while slightly below maximum since they're not as critical as Marrakech.

        - Public: 100

        - Rationale: Ottoman defensive pact expires in 1 turn - this is CRITICAL emergency security. Their 4,743 military strength fights Portugal and Carthage alongside me. With two wars ongoing and Portugal threatening cultural victory in 11 turns (Rome), losing Ottoman support would open multiple fronts during the science race. Maximum 100/100 relations preserve this alliance and ensure defensive pact renewal. Suleiman has 13 World Congress votes - losing his support would be devastating. I rely on Ottoman military presence to cover my flanks while focusing on science production.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380
        
        ### Portugal
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Portugal at 4/7 cultural victory is the existential threat. Rome is now at 84% Portuguese influence (11 turns from Influential) - if Portugal influences Rome, they reach 5/7 and are just 2 civs from cultural victory. My spy network in Lisbon, Braga, Gades, and Goa must complete spy missions for science boosts. The Marrakech quest for spy mission against Maria I provides 842 Science (~42% of a technology) - every beaker matters in this race. Maximum -100 hostility prevents any peace offers, maintains aggressive AI posture against Portuguese infrastructure, and prioritizes damaging their tourism output while I race for Information Era technologies.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Rome is my vassal with Order ideology. CRITICAL SITUATION: Rome is at 84% Portuguese influence (11 turns from Influential). If Portugal influences Rome, they reach 5/7 cultural victory. Maximum 100/100 relations ensure Rome stays loyal, generates maximum culture/tourism resistance against Portuguese influence, and maintains productivity as a vassal. Rome's military (2,775 strength) supports my wars. Every turn of delay against Portuguese influence matters for my science victory race.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380

        - Public: 100

        - Rationale: Venice has 63% diplomatic victory (28 votes) and is at war with Portugal (14% war score). Venice captured Bratislava from Portugal and just proposed Venetian Sphere of Influence: Bratislava in World Congress. They bought my Penicillin tech for 3610 gold, giving me needed funds. Venice fighting Portugal helps slow Portuguese cultural victory by disrupting Portuguese trade routes and forcing them to divert resources. Maximum 100/100 relations ensure they continue anti-Portugal measures and potentially support my interests in World Congress.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380

        - Rationale: Carthage remains at war with me at 8% war score in my favor. Just killed their Lancer at Panormus and damaged their Ironclad (per event logs showing CombatResult with Lancer death). They're Freedom ideology while I'm Order. Keeping maximum -100 hostility ensures AI continues aggressive prosecution of this war without diverting main focus from Portugal threat. However, Carthage is secondary priority compared to Portuguese cultural victory threat - if I need to make peace to focus on Portugal, the AI will consider it with current war score.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380
        
        ### America
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: America will be Influenced by Portugal next turn (98% - 1 turn remaining) as per previous reports. While this tourism damage is inevitable, maintaining maximum 100/100 relations prevents America from becoming hostile, keeps trade routes and Open Borders active for science/gold yields, and prevents secondary diplomatic complications. America remains useful as a trade partner and buffer state even after being Influenced.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380

        - Public: 100

        - Rationale: Marrakech city-state has the critical quest: 'Conduct a Spy Mission against Maria I' with reward of 338 Influence + 842 Science. This science is worth approximately 42% of a technology - in a race where Portugal could win in 11 turns (Rome), every beaker matters. Maximum 100/100 relations ensure the AI prioritizes completing this quest with my spy in Lisbon. Marrakech is also Ottoman ally fighting our enemies, providing additional military coordination value.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Turn 379 emergency situation - Portugal at 4/7 cultural victory with Rome at 84% (11 turns from Influential). All current strategic parameters remain optimal: Spaceship GrandStrategy, Production/Science/Spaceship/Espionage at 100, Mobilization at 100 for military production, Happiness at 100 to prevent collapse. Robotics 12 turns from completion leads to Rocketry → Nuclear Fission path for spaceship components. Iron Curtain policy remains critical for +200% trade route production. Ottoman defensive pact expires in 1 turn - current 100/100 relations should ensure renewal. Venice at 100/100 continues anti-Portugal war.             - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Catholicism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1

        You, Pocatello (leader of The Shoshone, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 380.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Rocketry: Allows you to build the Guided Missile, a one-time use unit specialized in damaging City garrisons.
        Completing it would unlock: Nuclear Fission, Radar
      
      ## Policies
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: keep-status-quo
      - rationale: Turn 380 assessment confirms all strategic parameters remain optimal. Portugal at 4/7 cultural victory with Rome falling in ~11 turns, but The Shoshone (34 turns) and Ottomans (71 turns) provide a realistic ~60+ turn window to achieve science victory. Ottoman defensive pact expired this turn with bilateral relations maxed at 100/100 — staff must prioritize immediate renewal. Robotics completes in 11 turns and Iron Curtain policy in 12 turns, both critical to the spaceship timeline. Washakie completed Nuclear Power Plant and Moson Kahni completed Tidal Power Plant, strengthening base production. Western fleet destroyed Carthaginian Lancer and Rifleman at Panormus; Carthage remains a secondary priority. Espionage rush must continue toward completing the Marrakech (842 Science) and Vancouver (533 Science) spy quests against Maria I. Flavors remain locked: Production/Science/Spaceship/Espionage/Mobilization/Happiness at 100, Defense at 80, Offense at 70. No flavor or grand-strategy changes are required.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Rocketry
        - Rationale: Robotics completes in 11 turns. Rocketry is the essential next technology as it unlocks Guided Missile and leads to Nuclear Fission → Advanced Ballistics → Satellites → Apollo Program, forming the critical path for science victory. Reconfirming Rocketry as the queued next research prevents any AI drift toward less urgent technologies such as Combined Arms.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Iron Curtain
        - Rationale: Policy selection available in 12 turns. Iron Curtain remains the optimal Order tenet for this stage of the science race. With 6+ internal trade routes currently active and 14 city connections, the +200% food/production from internal trade routes and +5 production/gold per city connection will generate roughly +400 sustained production per turn — far exceeding the one-time Great Person value of Spaceflight Pioneers over the 50+ turn production window. Free Courthouse on annexation also preserves gold for emergency rush-buying of spaceship components later.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the situation carefully.
            
            Current state (Turn 380):
            - I'm playing as The Shoshone (Pocatello)
            - Portugal is at 4/7 cultural victory, with Rome at 85% influence (rising, 11 turns to Influential)
            - I'm in the Information Era, researching Robotics (11 turns remaining)
            - I have 14 cities, good production/science base
            - I'm at war with Portugal and Carthage
            - Ottoman defensive pact expired (TurnsRemaining: 0 on deal #0)
            
            Key observations from events:
            1. Ottoman defensive pact expired - this is critical. I had 100/100 relations with Ottomans but the defensive pact deal shows TurnsRemaining: 0. I need to ensure it gets renewed.
            2. Combat with Carthage: killed their Lancer and Rifleman near Panormus
            3. I've upgraded several Gatling Guns to Machine Guns and Field Guns to Artillery
            4. Washakie built Nuclear Power Plant - excellent for production
            5. Moson Kahni built Tidal Power Plant
            6. Rome built Zoo
            
            Looking at victory progress:
            - Portugal: 4/7 influential, Rome at 85% (11 turns) - if Portugal influences Rome, they get 5/7
            - Venice: 63% diplomatic victory (28 votes, needs 44)
            - My science victory: Researching Robotics, need to get to Apollo Program
            
            Diplomatic situation:
            - Ottomans: Defensive pact expired (TurnsRemaining: 0). Their opinion of me is 100 public. They are fighting Portugal. I need maximum relations to get defensive pact renewed.
            - America: Influenced by Portugal next turn (98% -> now probably Influenced since events show turn 380 completed)
            - Rome: My vassal, 85% Portuguese influence. Need to keep loyal.
            - Venice: Friendly, at war with Portugal, bought my tech.
            - Carthage: At war, secondary priority.
            
            Spy situation:
            - Agent Hupia in Lisbon (network 600) - Gathering Intelligence
            - Agent Mugwayan in Goa (network 418) - Gathering Intelligence
            - Agent Tuziyammo in Gades (network 792) - Gathering Intelligence
            - Need to complete spy missions for science boosts and city-state quests
            
            World Congress in 5 turns:
            - Proposals: Decolonization (Portugal), Venetian Sphere of Influence: Bratislava (Venice), Repeal Open Door: Helsinki (Ottomans)
            - Active: Sanction on Portugal (good), Passport System (bad for me - boosts tourism by 50%), Scholars In Residence
            - I should support Venice's proposals and Ottomans' repeal
            
            Current strategy check:
            - Next tech: Rocketry (set previously, Robotics completes in 11 turns)
            - Next policy: Iron Curtain (in 12 turns)
            - Grand Strategy: Spaceship
            - Flavors: Production/Science/Spaceship/Espionage at 100, Happiness at 100, etc.
            
            Wait, I need to check what happened with America. The previous report said America would be Influenced by Portugal next turn (98%). Looking at the current cultural victory data... actually the provided data still shows America at 98% or the old data? Let me check the cultural victory section again.
            
            Looking at Portugal's influences:
            - America: Influential (119%) - Rising (tourism/culture ratio 443%)
            So America is already Influenced by Portugal. That means Portugal has America, Venice, Carthage, Ethiopia = 4/7. Rome at 85% (11 turns). The Shoshone at 67% (34 turns). Ottomans at 58% (71 turns).
            
            Wait, the data says Portugal has 4 Influential civs. Let me recount:
            - Venice: Influential (116%)
            - Carthage: Influential (132%)
            - Ethiopia: Influential (103%)
            - America: Influential (119%)
            Yes, 4/7. Rome is next at 85% (11 turns). If Rome falls, Portugal reaches 5/7. Then they need 2 more (The Shoshone and Ottomans).
            
            The race is: can I get science victory before Portugal gets cultural victory?
            
            Science path: Robotics (11 turns) -> Rocketry -> Nuclear Fission -> Advanced Ballistics -> Satellites -> Apollo Program -> spaceship parts. That's many technologies.
            
            Wait, I need to reconsider. Portugal could win in as few as 11 turns if they influence Rome, then me (34 turns) and Ottomans (71 turns). But actually, they need to influence ALL remaining civs. With Rome, that's 5/7. Then The Shoshone (34 turns) and Ottomans (71 turns). So maybe 71 turns minimum? But tourism can accelerate.
            
            Actually wait - cultural victory requires influencing ALL civs, not just 7. Let me re-read... "CivsNeeded: 7" and Portugal has 4 Influential. So they need 7 total? No, there are 8 civs total (The Shoshone, Ottomans, Venice, Carthage, Ethiopia, America, Rome, Portugal). Portugal needs to influence all 7 others. Currently 4/7. Rome next. Then The Shoshone and Ottomans remain.
            
            Actually looking again: "CivsNeeded: 7" - yes, 7 other civs. Portugal has 4. Needs 3 more: Rome, The Shoshone, Ottomans. Rome in ~11 turns. The Shoshone in ~34 turns. Ottomans in ~71 turns. Unless something changes (like they get more tourism or my culture drops).
            
            Passport System is active (+50% tourism modifier for all civs), which helps Portugal.
            
            I need to accelerate my science victory. Let me think about what to change.
            
            1. Technology: Rocketry is already set. This unlocks Guided Missile and path to Nuclear Fission. But wait - is Rocketry the best next tech? Let me think...
               - Robotics leads to what? It allows building spaceship parts maybe? No, in Civ V, you need Apollo Program first, then techs for spaceship parts. Actually in Vox Populi it might be different.
               - Rocketry unlocks Guided Missile, Nuclear Fission, Radar
               - Nuclear Fission unlocks Nuclear Power Plant (+50% production) and Manhattan Project
               - I already have Nuclear Power Plants (Washakie just built one). So Nuclear Fission is partly useful for the production boost? Wait, do I already have the tech for Nuclear Power Plant? Yes, Washakie built one this turn. So I already have Nuclear Fission? No wait... looking at city report: Washakie built "Nuclear Power Plant". Looking at my research: CurrentResearch is Robotics. So how did I build Nuclear Power Plant? Maybe it doesn't require Nuclear Fission in Vox Populi, or the report is slightly off. Actually, Nuclear Power Plant might require Nuclear Fission. Let me not worry about this - the point is my tech path is already set.
            
            Actually wait, I need to re-check. The options say technologies available are Combined Arms and Rocketry. But my current research is Robotics. How do I already have Nuclear Power Plant then? Maybe the building list includes "Nuclear Power Plant" but I might have had the tech already and just finished the building. Or maybe it's a different building. Let me check my techs: I have 66 technologies. The report says CurrentResearch: Robotics. It's possible I already have Nuclear Fission and the Nuclear Power Plant just finished construction.
            
            Actually, re-reading the options more carefully:
            - Combined Arms: Allows Tank
            - Rocketry: Allows Guided Missile, unlocks Nuclear Fission and Radar
            
            If Rocketry unlocks Nuclear Fission, then I DON'T have Nuclear Fission yet. But Washakie built a Nuclear Power Plant? Maybe the name is different or Vox Populi changed requirements. Or perhaps "Nuclear Power Plant" comes from a different tech. I'll trust my previous strategy which said Rocketry -> Nuclear Fission -> Advanced Ballistics.
            
            2. Policy: Iron Curtain in 12 turns. This is good. +200% internal trade routes, free courthouses, city connections +5 gold/production.
            
            3. Relationships:
               - Ottomans: Defensive pact expired. I must ensure I maintain maximum relations (100/100) so their AI wants to renew. Looking at current deal, Defensive Pact had TurnsRemaining: 0. This means it expired. My staff opinion shows "We have made a Defensive Pact!" but the deal expired. This is critical because Ottomans have 5,197 military strength fighting Portugal and Carthage.
               - However, looking at the TheirOpinionOfUs section for Ottomans, they desire friendly relations and have 100 opinion. So defensive pact should be renewable. But I should keep relations at maximum.
               
            Actually, looking at the DiplomaticDeals for Ottomans:
            - #0: WeGive: Defensive Pact, TheyGive: Defensive Pact + 7 Gold per Turn, TurnsRemaining: 0
            Yes, expired this turn. I need to get it renewed ASAP.
            
            Wait, the events don't show any new deal. But relations are maxed. Should I change anything? The staff AI should handle renewing deals with friendly civs. But to be safe, I'll keep relations at 100/100.
            
            4. Military situation:
               - Carthage war: killed units near Panormus. War score 10% in my favor.
               - Portugal war: 0% war score, but not much direct combat. Very far from my homeland.
               - I have strong navy (Dreadnoughts, Destroyers, Attack Submarines).
            
            5. Espionage:
               - Marrakech quest: spy mission against Maria I for 842 science
               - Vancouver quest: spy mission against Maria I for 533 science
               - I have spies in Lisbon and Gades. Need them to complete missions.
            
            Now, what changes should I make?
            
            The previous strategy was:
            - Rocketry next tech
            - Iron Curtain next policy
            - Spaceship grand strategy
            - Max Production/Science/Spaceship/Espionage/Happiness flavors
            - 100/100 Ottomans, Rome, Venice, America, Marrakech, Vancouver
            - -100/100 Portugal, Carthage
            
            I think the strategy is still sound. But let me think if anything needs adjustment.
            
            One thing: America is now Influenced by Portugal (was inevitable). Should I change America relations? Probably keep at 100/100 to maintain trade routes and prevent them from becoming hostile. Open Borders and trade routes still give me yields.
            
            Another thing: Should I lower Defense and Offense slightly to focus more on infrastructure? No, I have two wars ongoing.
            
            Actually, looking at my military strength (8,078) vs Portugal (12,370) and Carthage (2,370). Portugal is stronger militarily. But they're fighting Venice and Ottomans too.
            
            For spaceship victory, I need:
            - Apollo Program (needs Satellites, which needs Rocketry -> Advanced Ballistics or something)
            - Actually the tech tree in Vox Populi might differ. Let me think about what I know:
              - Rocketry unlocks Nuclear Fission and Radar
              - Nuclear Fission probably unlocks Advanced Ballistics? Or does Rocketry unlock something else?
              - Satellites is needed for Apollo Program
            
            Wait, the previous rationale said: "Nuclear Fission critical for Nuclear Power Plants (+50% production) which will significantly boost spaceship part production". But Washakie already built a Nuclear Power Plant. Maybe I misread and it's a different building, or maybe I already have the tech. Looking at buildings in Washakie: "Nuclear Power Plant" is listed under ImportantBuildings. So I do have it. Maybe Nuclear Fission was already researched and the previous rationale was slightly wrong, or Vox Populi has different requirements.
            
            Actually, looking at my CurrentResearch: Robotics. Technologies: 66. Let's see what techs Venice (65) and Ottomans (63) have. I'm slightly ahead. Let me not overthink the exact tech tree.
            
            Should I change research? The options only show Combined Arms and Rocketry. Combined Arms gives Tank. Rocketry gives Guided Missile and leads to Nuclear Fission/Radar. For science victory, Rocketry seems better as it progresses toward Information Era.
            
            One potential issue: I'm in the Information Era but researching Robotics. The Apollo Program might require different techs. Actually, looking at the victory progress, Science Victory says "Unlocked in the Information era". I'm already in the Information Era! So maybe I can already build the Apollo Program? No, typically you need specific techs.
            
            Wait, my cities don't show Apollo Program in production. So I need more techs.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully - maybe I should consider changing policy. Iron Curtain is in 12 turns. Is there a better policy available?
            
            Options include:
            - Socialist Realism (Order Level 3)
            - Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3) 
            - Iron Curtain (Order Level 3)
            - Communism (Order Level 1)
            - People's Army (Order Level 1)
            - Patriotic War (Order Level 1)
            - Rights of Man (Rationalism)
            - Double Agents (Order Level 1)
            - Imperium (Authority)
            - Colonialism (Imperialism)
            - Liberty (Progress)
            - Expertise (Progress)
            - Division of Labor (Industry)
            - Foreign Service (Statecraft)
            - Trade Confederacy (Statecraft)
            - Artistry (New Branch)
            - Fealty (New Branch)
            - And many more
            
            I currently have 5 Order policies adopted: Hero of the People, Academy of Sciences, Dictatorship of the Proletariat, Workers' Faculties, Peace, Land, Bread. I need 2 more for Level 3 tenets.
            
            Iron Curtain gives: Free Courthouse, +200% Food/Production from internal trade routes, +5 Gold and Production from city connections. With 14 cities and many internal trade routes, this is massive production.
            
            Spaceflight Pioneers gives: Free Great Engineer and Great Scientist, finish spaceship parts with Great Engineers, 100 science from expending Great Person. This is also amazing for science victory.
            
            Which is better? The previous rationale said Iron Curtain is better because "production infrastructure must be established before Great Engineer rushing becomes viable." But with 14 cities, many already have factories and production buildings, maybe Spaceflight Pioneers is now viable?
            
            Actually, looking at my city reports:
            - Rome: ProductionPerTurn: 436
            - Washakie: 435
            - Moson Kahni: 564
            - Harar: 369
            - Goshute: 398
            These are decent but not exceptional for spaceship parts (which usually need 1500+ production each).
            
            If I take Spaceflight Pioneers, I get a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. The Great Engineer can rush one spaceship part (saving ~1000+ production). The Great Scientist gives a tech boost. Plus future Great Engineers can rush parts.
            
            But wait - can I even build spaceship parts yet? I don't have Apollo Program. So having Great Engineers now might be premature.
            
            Iron Curtain boosts my economy immediately and permanently. With ~10+ internal trade routes and 14 city connections, that's huge. My internal trade routes already show 30-37 production each. +200% would make them 90-111 production each. If I have 5-6 internal trade routes, that's 300-450 extra production per turn. Plus city connections give +5 production each = 70 production. This is massive and helps build Apollo Program and all the spaceship parts faster.
            
            Actually, internal trade routes currently give:
            - Moson Kahni -> Te-Moak: 37.5 production
            - Moson Kahni -> Washakie: 30 production
            - Pohokwi -> Timbisha: 31.25 production
            - Moson Kahni -> Agaidika: 37.5 production
            - Rome -> Antium: 27 production
            And some give food (Moson Kahni -> Harar: 52.75 food)
            
            So internal trade routes are giving roughly 163 production base. +200% = 489 production. Plus city connections +5 each = 70. Total ~560 extra production/turn across the empire. This could dramatically accelerate spaceship part building once Apollo Program is built.
            
            So Iron Curtain is probably correct.
            
            Now, should I change any flavors?
            
            Current flavors:
            - Offense: 70
            - Defense: 80
            - Mobilization: 100
            - CityDefense: 55
            - MilitaryTraining: 50
            - Recon: 15
            - Ranged: 50
            - Mobile: 45
            - Nuke: 80
            - UseNuke: 75
            - Naval: 70
            - NavalRecon: 30
            - NavalGrowth: 20
            - NavalTileImprovement: 30
            - Air: 85
            - AirCarrier: 60
            - Antiair: 55
            - Airlift: 75
            - Expansion: 0
            - Growth: 80
            - TileImprovement: 75
            - Infrastructure: 95
            - Production: 100
            - WaterConnection: 25
            - Gold: 40
            - Science: 100
            - Culture: 15
            - Happiness: 100
            - GreatPeople: 100
            - Wonder: 5
            - Religion: 15
            - Diplomacy: 20
            - Spaceship: 100
            - Espionage: 100
            
            I think most of these are good. Production, Science, Spaceship, Espionage at 100 are critical. Happiness at 100 prevents collapse. Mobilization at 100 for war production.
            
            One concern: Culture at 15 is very low. Portuguese tourism is the threat. But I'm not going for cultural victory - I need science victory. Low culture means I'm more vulnerable to Portuguese influence. Should I increase Culture to resist?
            
            If Portugal influences Rome in 11 turns, they get 5/7. Then they need me and Ottomans. I'm at 67% (34 turns). If I increase culture output, I can slow this down. But my PolicyBranches show I have Tradition, Progress, Authority, Rationalism, Statecraft, Industry, Imperialism, Order. That's a lot of branches.
            
            My CulturePerTurn: 1686. That's decent but Portugal's tourism is 951.75/turn against me. My tourism modifier might be bad.
            
            Actually, the cultural victory screen shows Portugal influences The Shoshone at 67%, rising, 34 turns to Influential, tourism/culture ratio 260%. This means their tourism against me is about 2.6x my culture. To delay them, I'd need massive culture increases.
            
            Could I switch to a more culture-focused approach? Probably not - I need science for spaceship.
            
            But there's another option: if I can't win science victory in time, maybe I should try to cripple Portugal militarily. They have 12,370 military strength. I have 8,078. Venice has 6,607. Ottomans have 5,197. Together we're stronger.
            
            Could I use my navy to attack Portuguese coastal cities? Lisbon, Porto, Braga, Coimbra, Funchal, Goa, etc. are all coastal. I have a strong navy with Dreadnoughts, Attack Submarines, Destroyers. But Portugal is far away - on the other side of the map. I'd need carriers and airpower.
            
            Actually, looking at my units, I have Bombers, Triplanes, but mostly land units. My navy is strong but getting it to Portugal would take many turns.
            
            Wait - looking at my city Panormus (x:7, y:6), that's actually quite far west. And I see Djenne (x:5, y:15) which is near Venice. My naval units are moving around x:70-74 (east). Portugal is at x:23-33. Venice is at x:5-20. So the western theater is where Venice and Carthage are.
            
            Looking at zone 11164 (Panormus), I have Destroyers, Rifleman, Triplane, Bomber there. Zone -11164 (sea near Panormus) has Destroyers, Dreadnought, Attack Submarines. So I do have a western fleet.
            
            But Portugal's cities are at x:23-33, y:12-47. Panormus is at x:7, y:6. That's still far.
            
            There's also the fact that I'm at war with Carthage near Panormus. Portugal is further east from Carthage.
            
            Actually, looking at Carthage city locations: Carthage x:23,y:7, Utique x:20,y:14, Saguntum x:17,y:10, Carthago Nova x:11,y:5. Portugal: Lisbon x:23,y:24, Porto x:26,y:27, Braga x:29,y:23. So Portugal is adjacent to/near Carthage territory.
            
            Maybe I could work with Venice (who is at war with Portugal) to pressure Portugal? Venice captured Bratislava from Portugal. But Venice is Freedom ideology and I'm Order.
            
            Given the time pressure, I think science victory is still the best path but with a very narrow window. If I can get Iron Curtain and boost production massively, I might rush spaceship parts.
            
            But wait - do I even know what techs are needed for spaceship parts? In standard Civ V:
            - Apollo Program (requires Satellites?)
            - SS Cockpit requires Nanotechnology or something
            - SS Engine requires Ion Propulsion or something
            - SS Stasis Chamber requires Nanotechnology
            - SS Thrusters require Robotics or something
